# S1 郊野 · 古柳: the countryside: the map in words

Generated by `tools/scroll_map.mjs S1 text` from `map/strokes/S1.json` (stroke points on the scan) and `map/frames/S1.json`. Never edit this file: change the strokes or the frame and regenerate. Scan range u 29050–37610.

## How to read it

- **Station** is metres along the route (`S1.road.main`) from the datum, where the route crosses the seam with S2 (u 29050): S1.road.main's centre-line point 0, exactly S2.road.main's last centre point (281.07, 16.29 m; scan (29050, 730), measured by S2; copied from map/strokes/S2.json by merge_fixes.mjs on every run). Station +314.87 there, S2's route station at that point (sections/canal/layout.json), so stations run on.. **+ is east** (picture right); **− is west**. The scroll is read from right to left, so the walk below starts at the east end.
- **Side** is metres north (N, away from the scroll's viewer) or south (S, toward the viewer) of the route's centre line. **H** is height above the ground.
- Directions: E = picture right, N = away from the viewer. "faces W" means the body faces west.
- Positions come from points measured on the strokes. "(est)" marks a point estimated because the strokes hide it (feet behind a rail, a corner behind a tree). Metres also rest on the frame's assumptions below.
- Painted figures are about 1.3× life size against the architecture; positions use foot points, so this doesn't move anyone.

## Frame and assumptions

- **F1** covers u 28900–37610: u = 8248 + 77·X + dx(u)·D; v = 1565 − 51.25·D − 77·H, where the receding direction dx(u) drifts along the scroll: -51.6 at u 29050 (linear between, constant beyond).
  - Derived: Seed (mapping_rules.md, frame, "S3, S2, S1"; brief_countryside.md, The frame): S5's origin (S5.str.moat_bridge:base.sw, scan 8248, 1565), 77 px per metre across and in height, 51.25 px of v per metre of depth (map/README.md, Frames, One world across sections), as map/frames/S2.json. Covers u 28900-37610: the area from 150 px west of the seam to the silk's end.
  - Derived: Seam knot [29050, -51.6]: S2's dx at u 29050 (S2's last knot [28121, -51.6], constant to the seam; map/frames/S2.json, option A, the measured knots). A point on the seam maps to the same metres in both sections: S1's painted area's west corners are S2's east corners (map_layout: se (267.21, -4.39), ne (290.62, 30.54)).
  - Derived: NO MEASURED KNOT. Every unit searched its range for a ground-level edge running straight back (furniture, a wall's foot, a fence, a ditch, a deck, a field's rows) and found none (gen/map/S1/reports/structures_w.md "The receding-edge table", structures_e.md "Receding-edge table", t1.md "Receding edges"): the stall counter and the small table are drawn frontal; every fence, hedge, bank, field line and the 菜地's rows run along the scroll (slopes 0 to +0.03); no farmhouse shows a side wall (fences and hedges hide the walls, the roofs are long side on or gable-on); the threshing floor has no drawn edge; eaves and ridges run level. Candidates, all REJECTED: the farmhouse hall's west hip (29976, 1050) -> (30080, 925), slope -1.20 (dx +43; a hip runs at 45 deg in plan, and eave-to-ridge level); the stall shed's east hip (+0.85, a hip); the farmhouse wing's two wall feet, +0.44 and -0.34 (the wing is drawn turned, the two faces give no single dx: solved as a turned box they give dx +126 or -92); the shed on posts' east and west post feet (31445, 1235) -> (31357, 1190), +0.51 (dx -100, up-left, if the west post is a back post; more likely it stands behind the tray); the small bridge's posts paired south to north, (36352, 1090) -> (36315, 1062) +0.76 (dx -67) or (36236, 1094) -> (36315, 1062) -0.41 (dx +126): the pairing is unreadable; the stream's west-bank waterline (36125, 1058) -> (36030, 1255), slope -2.1 (dx +24; a bank line curves); the barrow lying tipped (+0.39, a thing lying at an angle); the far raised path and diag_ne (+0.88, +0.91: they run north-west to south-east on the ground, not straight back); the sedan's poles (-0.29: the sedan heads west-south-west). The two post pairings that can be read at all lean up-left, the sign of S2's east knot; nothing at ground level contradicts it.
  - Derived: HELD: dx = -51.6 from the seam to the silk's end (a constant drift needs no ramp: every row keeps 1.30 m of X per 100 px of u). Against a frontal reading (dx 0), a point's X moves -dx·D/77: 20.47 m east at the silk's top (D 30.5 m), 2.94 m west at its bottom edge (D -4.4 m), 7.3-10.92 m east on the route (D 10.9-16.3 m). The countryside is drawn square on, so the receding direction cannot be checked against any wall here (questions: what the alternatives would change).
  - Assumed: East-west and height 77 px per metre, depth 51.25 px of v per metre, as every section (S5). Things up the silk are painted smaller (questions: the diminution); positions come only from the map (BUILDING.md rule 5).
  - Assumed: dx is S2's seam value, -51.6, held constant over the whole of S1 because no ground-level edge recedes anywhere in it (derivation). It is the painter's drift at S2's east wharf carried on, not a reading of S1.
  - Assumed: Painted figure size is not a scale: S1's foreground travellers are 171-189 px tall (2.2-2.5 m at 77 px/m), 1.8 times S4's road figures, and the party's porters high up 80-115 px. Place by foot point.
  - Assumed: The ground is one level (ground, H 0) everywhere it is not water: every bank, terrace edge and field line is a ground line at H 0. Each painted band is a bank one step higher to the north (35-90 px of face in the foreground, 5-20 px far up), and its rise is a build-time choice of the ground pilot (questions), not a frame height: the frame maps each stroke at H 0, so a bank top is placed as if it were level with its foot.
  - Assumed: Water: the stream and the pale band (if it is water) are the support `stream`, H -0.7, measured at the bridge. A ground line drawn on the water's edge (a bank's foot that is the shore, the land tongues' edges) stands on `stream` (support_by, support), so the same stroke maps to one place; the near bank's top (the shores' shore_s) stays on the ground: the water at -0.7 lies 1.05 m north of it, the bank's slope between.
  - Assumed: Riders: a rider's foot point is the ground under his seat (the units' convention), so his animal's support is H 0 and the builder seats him on the animal.

- Support **ground**: H 0 m (the countryside ground: the road, the farmyards, the fields and banks (one level, see assumptions). 216 entities).
- Support **water**: H -0.7 m (COPIED FROM map/frames/S4b.json (one H for the whole 汴河, Phase 0), as S3's and S2's frames copied it. The 汴河 does not reach S1 (S2.water.bian_river_e's north shore turns south under the massive willow and meets the silk's bottom edge at (28968, 1780, est); no S1 entity stands on it): the key is kept so the joined world has one water level. S4b's derivation: MEASURED AS A BOUND (conf low), the river agent's reading (gen/map/S4b/reports/river.md, Water level): H = -(v_waterline - v_ground) / 77 at the same u, the ground at street level (H 0), at the three places where flat ground meets the first water on S4b's moorings: M1 the bank path at the gangplank, ground (14778, 401) under the porter's feet and the plank's foot, over the first ripple swirl (14778, 456): 55 px, -0.71; M2a the top-left building's front wall foot (14920, 166) over the landing's lower wash edge (14920, 224): 58 px, -0.75; M2b the same wall foot (14990, 151) over the landing's edge (14990, 203): 52 px, -0.68. Spread 0.07 m; M2a and M2b (a wall foot, the narrowest bank) are the surest. The bank is a wash-drawn slope with no vertical face, stair, revetment or jetty, so each reading is an upper bound on the depth: a landing s m deep puts the water 0.67 s m higher (plausible range -0.3 to -0.75). S3's river agents found no vertical face either; river_e's bounds at the far-bank hall on posts (18446-18542: -0.86 to -1.0) fit. 1 m of H moves every boat and shore point 1.5 m north or south (77 / 51.25). One H for the whole 汴河: S3, S2 and S1 copy it; a revetment, stair or abutment foot read later replaces it only with the lead's agreement. Replaces the seed's -1.5 (S5's moat, assumed).).
- Support **stream**: H -0.7 m (MEASURED (two stroke points each, structures_e; gen/map/S1/reports/structures_e.md, "The stream's water level"): the stream under the small bridge is not the 汴河 and has its own level. The bridge's deck meets both bank tops level ((36125, 1052) at the west bank, (36608, 1032) at the east), so the deck is at ground level (H 0), and its posts stand in the water: the deck's south edge over the post foot straight below, (36540, 1036) -> (36540, 1090), 54 px, -0.70 m; (36352, 1038) -> (36352, 1090), 52 px, -0.68 m; and the west bank's top (36125, 1052) over its waterline (36110, 1110), 58 px, -0.75 m. Mean -0.71, spread 0.07; the shorter posts (feet v 1062-1075) would give -0.3 to -0.5 if their feet were at the waterline, but they read as a north row (their feet further back, not higher). That it equals the 汴河's -0.7 is a coincidence of two separate readings. Used by S1.water.stream_36500, S1.water.wash_band_34400 (the pale band, if it is water: conf low), the bridge's post feet (support_by bays), the sampan and its mooring pole, the land tongues and the shoal, and the bank feet that are the water's shore lines (support_by bank_foot: S1.grd.bank_36900, bank_36060, dyke_36380, and, with the pale band's water reading, bank_33650 and bank_35200). 1 m of H moves a point 1.5 m in depth).
- Support **bank slope**: H -0.38 m (DERIVED from where each foot stands on the stream's west bank face (S1.grd.bank_36060: bank_top on the ground, its foot the waterline at the stream's -0.7): a straight face, so H = -0.7 x f with f = (v_foot - v_top) / (v_waterline - v_top) at the foot's u. S1.tree.bare_elm_twin_35920: foot v 1200, the face's top v 1140 and waterline v 1250 at u 35920, f 0.55, H -0.38; S1.tree.bare_elm_tall_36070: foot v 1170, top 1067, waterline 1188 at u 36070, f 0.85, H -0.59 (its foot is drawn 10 px above the waterline: at the water's edge). An H_profile in u because only these two trees use it (read at each foot's own u; flat beyond). Without it the tall elm's foot maps inside the stream).
- Support **perched: S1.tree.elm_slender_33906**: H 8.96 m (derived like S2's and S4b's perched birds: the thing at its tree's depth, H = (v_base - v_thing) / 77: the nest S1.prop.nest_33900 (v 245) over the tree's foot (33906, 935)).
- Support **perched: S1.tree.bare_elm_twin_34552**: H 6.9 m (derived: the nest S1.prop.nest_34445 (v 405) over the tree's foot (34552, 936)).
- Support **perched: S1.tree.bare_elm_tall_34703**: H 7.73 m (derived: the mean of its two nests, S1.prop.nest_34680 (v 300) and S1.prop.nest_34757 (v 360), over the tree's foot (34703, 925, est): 8.12 and 7.34 m each, so each nest maps about 0.6 m north or south of the trunk (on its branch)).
- Support **perched: S1.tree.elm_34110**: H 7.25 m (derived: the pale bird S1.ani.pale_bird_34250 (conf low; its feet est at v 345) over the tree's foot (34110, 903, est)).
- Support **animal S1.ani.horse_party_31023**: H 0 m (the party's rider S1.fig.rider_party_30988: his foot point (30988, 355, est) is the ground under his seat between the horse's hooves, so H 0; named so the rider's support is explicit (S3's lesson: riders need their animal's height)).
- Support **animal S1.ani.donkey_traveller_30216**: H 0 m (the first hooded traveller S1.fig.traveller_rider_30165: foot point (30165, 1500, est) on the line between the donkey's hooves, H 0).
- Support **animal S1.ani.donkey_traveller_30546**: H 0 m (the second hooded traveller S1.fig.traveller_rider_30495: foot point (30495, 1612, est) on the donkey's hoof line, H 0).

## Landmarks, east to west

- **the stream at the small bridge, opening into the misty water at the scroll's start** `S1.water.stream_36500`: stations +444.6 to +414.7; side 0.6 m N to 14.4 m S. The stream (not the 汴河) under the small bridge: plain pale silk with the bridge's posts standing in it and a small empty boat lying in it (t8), bounded by the big trees' bank on the west, the layered north bank on the north and east, and the dark near bank at the bottom; land tongues (spits) reach into it from the west. East of about u 36850 it fades into plain silk (mist) and the seals. Note: WATER, because the bridge's posts stand in it (feet v 1062-1098) and the boat (t8, u 36170-36440, v 1128-1215) lies in it, and it is drawn as blank silk the way the other open water is. shore_n: the west bank's foot at the big trees' roots (u 36030-36125), then hidden under the deck: the line along the foot of the donkey path's bank (v ~980) is est (the strip between that bank and the deck, v 980-1003, may be water, the inlet's head, or bank: conf low); then the foot of the north bank (u 36615-36870), then the foot of the bank layers to the east (est). Between that bank and the east spit S1.grd.spit_east_37060 a pale arm (v ~1085-1150, u 36880-37250) belongs to the water. shore_s: the top of the dark near bank along the bottom (v 1592-1625), which fades out east of u ~36850. WEST: at u ~36000 it joins the pale band S1.water.wash_band_34400 (conf low) between the land tongues and the near bank. The restyle paints all of it as dry ground with grass and a stone-edged channel; never follow it here. DEPTH for the frame (support stream): the deck meets the bank tops level, and its south edge stands 54 px over the post foot straight below at u 36540 ((36540, 1036) -> (36540, 1090)), 52 px at u 36352 ((36352, 1038) -> (36352, 1090)): H ≈ -0.7 m (54/77), range -0.5 to -0.8; the west bank top (36125, 1052) over its waterline (36110, 1110) gives 58 px, -0.75 m. SECOND PASS (start): EXTENTS. shore_s is the near bank's top edge (S1.grd.bank_south_34400, same points). It runs on east to about 37250 and fades there; it is not lost at 36850. Its west end stays at u 36000, where it meets S1.water.wash_band_34400; the near bank's dark band itself begins at about 35900. shore_n follows the north bank's lowest stroke, which runs on east between the seals to the silk's end (S1.grd.bank_36900, below). The seals are measured by red pixels (R-G > 45): u 37248-37503 at v 51-319, u 37396-37538 at v 805-1007, u 37362-37567 at v 1138-1351, u 37424-37584 at v 1597-1756. The strip v 1007-1138 is covered by none of them. East of about 37250 the water between the east spit (S1.grd.spit_east_37060) and the near bank's end is plain silk (mist): close the polygon there, at the builder's choice.
- **layered north bank east of the bridge** `S1.grd.bank_36900`: stations +444.6 to +432.7; side 0.6 m N to 2.7 m S. The stream's north bank east of the small bridge: layered dark wash bands stepping down from the donkey path to the water, with a rounded dark mound at u ~36880-36980; the big tree at the bridge's east end stands on it, and a bare tree with a leaning pole (t8) stands on it further east. Note: Read at zoom 1.45 (c_eastbank.jpg) and 0.9 (c_bridge_wide.jpg). A long dark band slopes from the tree roots (36620, 1060) to (36870, 1110); the rounded mound u 36870-36990, v 1000-1060; dark strokes at u 36950-37150, v 1060-1080. HIGHER SIDE: north. HEIGHT: 70-90 px of face (v ~1010-1025 -> 1080-1110); with the stream ~0.7 m below the bank tops (bridge), judged 0.7 m. Nothing under the seal (u 37250-37560) is guessed. SECOND PASS (start): the bank's lowest stroke runs on east past 37250, as one dark line at v ~1060-1085 (eastend_z2.jpg), to about 37570, 40 px short of the silk's end. No seal covers it: the seals are at v 805-1007 (u 37396-37538) and v 1138-1351 (u 37362-37567). Measured foot (dark to light, 24 px columns, 10 px bins): 37150 at 1080, 37250 at 1085, 37350 at 1077, 37400 at 1077, 37450 at 1076, 37500 at 1066, 37580 at ~1066 (weak). East of ~37150 the face above it is only this one stroke, so bank_top there is est, about 20 px above the foot. FRAME: its bank_foot stands on the support `stream` (support_by), the water's level: its foot is the same strokes as S1.water.stream_36500's shore_n from u 36615 east (the waterline); its bank_top stays on the ground, so the face falls 0.7 m to the water.
- **faint bank north of the road at the east end** `S1.grd.bank_37050`: stations +443.5 to +436.3; side 3.8 m N to 0.3 m N. A faint dark wash band on the ground north of the road at the scroll's start, behind the bare tree with the leaning pole: a low step in the ground rising north toward the mist. Note: Read at zoom 1.7 (c_ne.jpg): dark wash v 838-870 at u 36830-36990, continuing behind the tree at u 37150-37300 (v 830-870). Above it thin horizontal streaks at v ~700-720 (u 36800-37000) and then plain silk (mist). HIGHER SIDE: north. HEIGHT: 30 px of face; judged a low step (~0.3 m) or only texture. SECOND PASS (start): this is one layered ink-wash bank face. The strokes lie in three layers, v ~840-860, 880-916 and 932-968 at 37160-37400 (rootband_z25.jpg), with the trees' roots in them. The mapped bank_foot (868-870) is the lower edge of the top layer only. The face's sharp lower edge is the lowest layer's, from column profiles (20 px, 5 px bins): 36900 at 918 (110 -> 131), 36950 at 925, 37000 at 920, 37050 at 932, 37125 at 952, 37200 at 958, 37250 at 962, 37300 at 968, 37350 at 976, 37390 at 978. It runs to the upper-middle seal's west edge (u 37396, v 805-1007), not to 37240. So the bank is about 80-140 px of face, and both east trees stand on it: S1.tree.slender_bare_37018 (foot v ~852) high on the face, S1.tree.elm_bare_37140 (950) near its foot. Their support could be 'bank slope' if the frame gives one. S1.road.main's edge_n est (36850-37240: 900-958) already runs along this foot within about 10 px. The open strip below it (v ~975-1050) runs on to the seal at 37396, which bears on the road's east end (next change). FRAME AGENT: the two trees on this face (S1.tree.slender_bare_37018, S1.tree.elm_bare_37140) stay on `ground`: the frame's `bank slope` (H -0.35) is the stream bank's face half way down to the water, and this bank rises above the road, so it would put them too far north. The face's rise is the ground pilot's terrain (questions).
- **the country road from the market through the village, under the great willows and past the farmsteads to the charcoal train's path** `S1.road.main`: stations +442.7 to +313.9; side 7.0 m N to 6.3 m S. The walk through S1's west half, read from the open, trodden ground (no road is drawn in the countryside): from the market seam it runs east across the village ground in front of the stall shed (where the buffalo, the ox and the barrow are), over the open field where the runaway horse and its chasers run, then turns south between the farmhouse and the hut under a willow's hanging branches, down to the travellers' band of ground, runs east along the willow dyke (隋堤) among the great pollarded willows, and climbs north-east behind the massive willows toward the farmhouses' road. East of u 33000 (structures_e's join piece, spliced in the merge): The country road along the front of the farmsteads: a level strip of bare earth between the farmhouses' fences and the bank in front of them, past the open barn and along the south of the threshing floor, then north of the big bare trees and down the charcoal donkey train's path, which runs diagonally from the train's head (north-west) to its driver (south-east) on a low bank above the stream, and on east along the north bank until the seal and the mist take it. No road edge is drawn anywhere: the edges are where the trodden ground meets fence, bank or open ground. Note: POINT 0 = the datum, copied from S2.road.main's last centre point in gen/map/S2/parts/structures_w.json: (29050,730) est, with S2's edges there (edge_n 695, edge_s 1000) - the open ground in front of the 望楼饭铺 shed's east end and S1's stall. The EAST END (32850, 33000, 33150) copies structures_e's join piece S1.road.main_east point for point (centre 1000/985/965, edge_n 960/945/925, edge_s 1040/1025/1005), so the splice at 33000 is clean. EDGES, how read: west stretch (29050-30450) edge_n = the stall's front line (post foot v 664; t1's counter S1.prop.stall_counter_29430 at v 664-666) and the foot of the chase field's north bank (S1.grd.bank_30300); edge_s = under the bottom-left willow's crown (the walker at 29200 has his feet at about v 1060), the 菜地's north edge (S1.grd.field_vegetable_29790) and the farmhouse hall's back (placeholder). The turn south (30400-30720) is NOT painted as a path: it is the 300 px gap of open ground under hanging willow branches between the farmhouse (east end about 30400) and the hut's fence (west end 30712); all its points est. Lower stretch (30720-32300): edge_n = the hut fence's foot, the foot of S1.grd.bank_30750 and the willow dyke's front bank (S1.grd.bank_31660); edge_s = the tops of the next banks down (S1.grd.bank_30900, S1.grd.bank_31680). It passes south of the willows at 31548-31939 and north of the massive willow's trunk foot at about 32080 (hidden behind it). The CLIMB (32300-32850) to structures_e's road in front of the farmhouses (v about 920 at 33450) is not painted: it runs behind (north of) the massive trunk at 32470-32780, crossing S1.grd.bank_32445 and S1.grd.bank_32900; all est. OTHER READINGS of the route: (a) an upper way behind the hut and the great willows at v about 850-1000 (the willows' crowns hide that ground; it would skip the willows' feet, 隋堤烟柳's view); (b) the travellers' path (S1.road.path_30300) reached from the village ground through the 54 px gap between the 菜地's east end and the farmhouse wing (u 30002-30056, v 1130-1300): too narrow for a road; (c) under the bottom-left willow past the walker at 29200: nothing drawn. The sedan party's track (S1.road.path_30950) leaves the silk at its top and cannot carry the route east. MERGE: spliced at u 33000 with structures_e's join piece S1.road.main_east (dropped): points up to and including u 33000 are structures_w's, points east of it the piece's (identical at 32850/33000/33150, so the splice is clean). The east piece's own reading follows. HOW READ. u 33400-35250 (conf med): edge_n = the feet of the brushwood fences and hedges (v 882-918) and the barn's posts (v 867-880); edge_s = the top of the yard bank S1.grd.yard_bank_34400 (v 945-966; 925-935 at the threshing floor). u 36190-36900 (conf med): the centre is the line of the donkey train's hooves and drivers' feet (t8: head driver ~(36245, 766), donkeys at v 798, 830-841, 880-885, 903-917, 936-946, rear driver (36795, 925)); edge_s = the top of the dyke bank S1.grd.dyke_36380 under them; edge_n is est, ~50 px north of the centre. u 35250-36190: not painted as a path; est, joining the threshing floor to the train's head behind (north of) the big bare trees whose bases are at v ~1100-1230. u 32850-33300: under the willow crown, est; structures_w's reading of S1.road.main at u 33000-33150 decides, and the merge reconciles the overlap. East of the rear driver nothing marks it: it is est, bent to pass south of the roots of the bare tree with the leaning pole (t8, roots u 37030-37180, v 880-955) and north of the north bank's top. East end (37240, 975) est: from u 37250 a seal covers the strip (v 800-980) and beyond it is plain silk (mist), so nothing past it is recorded. WHY THIS IS THE MAIN ROAD: the charcoal donkey train, the painting's travellers here, walks it toward the city, and it is the only continuous level strip through the farmsteads; the bridge path S1.road.path_36380 has no readable continuation off the bridge. Width 40-80 px of v (about 1-1.5 m at 51.25 px/m). MERGE (for the frame and the builder): between u 35030 and 35420 the route's centre (v 905-895) and edge_n (v 860-845) run inside the south part of S1.grd.threshing_floor_35225 (v 800-930): the road skirts the floor along its south edge, and the two share that strip of open ground; nothing is painted to separate them. The two unpainted bends (30400-30720 south, 32300-32850 north) and the est stretch 35250-36190 are recorded choices of reading, not strokes. SECOND PASS (start): the note says 'from u 37250 a seal covers the strip (v 800-980)'. Measured, the seal at that height spans u 37396-37538, v 805-1007. The seal starting at 37248 is the top one (v 51-319). Between 37250 and 37396 the strip is painted open ground: the bank's foot (S1.grd.bank_37050, v 962-978) above it and the north bank's top (S1.grd.bank_36900, v ~1045-1060) below it. The est route can run on to the seal's edge, ending at 37390. That is OPTIONAL: it is still a reading through unpainted ground, and it adds about 2 m to the walkable east end. FRAME: point 0 of each line is S2.road.main's last point at u 29050, copied from map/strokes/S2.json on every run of merge_fixes.mjs (centre (29050, 730) measured by S2, edges (29050, 695) and (29050, 1000) est): the datum of map/frames/S1.json, station +314.87. FRAME: edge_n gets two points at the hut willow's bole, (31150, 1500, est) and (31250, 1500, est), between (31100, 1484) and (31300, 1470, est): with the bole's base moved to its drawn lower edge (31215, 1492, est; second pass willows) the old line ran about 20 px inside the bole. The centre (31150, 1530, est) is unchanged. FRAME (from the second pass farms, for the lead): from u 33400 to 34750 nine tree bases stand between edge_n and edge_s, five on or south of the centre (S1.tree.elm_forked_33433, elm_forked_33785, elm_tall_33865, elm_slender_33906, elm_twin_33936, elm_spreading_34030, bare_elm_twin_34552, bare_elm_twin_34640, bare_elm_tall_34703): the light strip between the fences' feet (v ~895-918) and the yard bank's top (v ~943-950) is the only level ground there, and the trees stand on it. The lines are kept as read (the strokes don't settle it): the walkable line weaves between the trunks (a build choice), or the lead moves the centre onto the bank top just south of the feet (v ~945-950).

A broad dark band of low bank in the water east of the bridge, south of the north bank, running east under the seals. Between it and the north bank a pale arm of water (v ~1085-1150). South of it plain silk: water or mist. SECOND PASS (start): measured to the lower-middle seal's west edge (u 37362, v 1138-1351). - **path over the small bridge** `S1.road.path_36380`: stations +436.2 to +421.7; side on the centre line to 4.9 m S. The path carried by the small plank bridge, from the west bank under the big bare trees across the inlet to the east bank, and up the bank to the main road east of the donkey driver. Note: On the bridge the edges are the deck lines of S1.str.small_bridge_36365 (u 36125-36608); the deck meets both banks level, so the whole path is at ground level. Off the bridge nothing is drawn: the west end stops at the west bank among the big trees' roots (est), and the east end climbs the north bank's top (S1.grd.bank_36900, v ~1000-1025) to meet S1.road.main_east at about (36840, 932) (est). Who walks it: nobody is painted on it.
- **near bank along the bottom of the silk** `S1.grd.bank_south_34400`: stations +435.4 to +369.8; side 6.2 m S to 16.6 m S. The near ground along the bottom of the silk: a dark line or band (the bank's face) at the south edge of the pale band, with streaked bare ground below it to the silk's edge; rocks break through it at u 34190-34820. East of u ~36850 it fades into plain silk (mist). Note: bank_top here is the line where the pale band meets the dark face (the shore, if the band is water); bank_foot the lower edge of the dark face. Read at zoom 0.6-1.35 (w_*.jpg, rocks.jpg, c_junction.jpg). u 34150-34850: the face is a broad dark band v 1504-1578 (the rocks' north side). u 34850-36000: only a faint line at v ~1590-1600 (est). u 36000-36850: a dark band v 1590-1680, clear. WHICH SIDE IS HIGHER cannot be read for sure: the dark face is visible, so by the rule used everywhere here it faces the viewer and the pale band (north) is higher than the near ground; if the pale band is water, this is simply the near shore and the ground south of it is the higher. Below it, to the silk's edge at v ~1786, streaked bare ground: lower_band_top is the top of a second dark band (v ~1662-1720, clear at u 33000-33300, est east of it). West of u 33000 these are structures_w's S1.grd.bank_32700 (v 1578-1614) and S1.grd.bank_32705 (v 1659-1714); their top line reads ~25 px higher than mine at u 33000-33150. MERGE: continues west of u 33000 as structures_w's S1.grd.bank_32700 (at u 33000: top 1580/1610, foot 1612/1640: structures_e reads the band about 30 px lower, so its top meets this bank's foot (both low)). SECOND PASS (start): the dark face runs on east of u 36850 with a clear top edge. The light-to-dark step was read on column profiles (32 px columns, 5 px bins, blur 1.5): 36600 at 1605/1620, 36700 at 1605/1630, 36850 at 1637 (weak), 36950 at 1632 (sharp, 123 -> 104), 37050 at 1640 (126 -> 111), 37150 at 1635 (weak), 37250 at 1630 (134 -> 126, fading). By 37300-37350 only a trace remains, so it ends at about 37250, under plain silk (mist). Its lower edge is clear only at 36950 (1665-1670). West of the bridge the top edge measures 1588 at 35900, 1578 at 36050, 1585 at 36200, 1605 at 36350 and 36500, and 1612 at 36600 (points replaced, within about 10 px of the old ones). West of about 35850 nothing is drawn. At 35450-35750 the whole lower half (v 1340-1770) is one even mid-tone with no step, and a vertical silk crease runs at u 35500-35520. So the est stretch 34850-35850 is a join across bare silk, not a reading of strokes. Below this face, at 36000-36950, a second dark band lies at v ~1700-1765, just above the silk's lower edge (v 1770): the east continuation of `lower_band_top` (not proposed as a line; it runs off the silk).

A long low plank bridge on thin wooden posts, its deck covered with brushwood or turf (rough dotted strokes on top), running east-west from the west bank under the big bare trees to the east bank at the next big tree; no railing. The line at its east end (u 36442-36446, v ~1125-1180) stops 40-60 px below this bridge's nearest post foot (36455, 1063); second pass: a mooring pole in the stream bed (S1.prop.mooring_pole_36444, low), not a rope to a post.

A long low house behind the open barn: a straight ridge and a roof of fine close strokes in rows (thatch, probably; no round tile ends are painted), with a small hip-and-gable end facing west; its east part, beyond the barn, shows an open front with a few posts over the threshing floor. Thin vertical strokes at u 35050-35100, v 750-830, under its east part are recorded apart as S1.str.posts_35075 (its front posts, or a lean-to or frame on the threshing floor). The slope is laid in rows of small scalloped marks, not the barn's slanted dashes (roofs_n.jpg, roofs_hp.jpg zoom 2.6). Those two signs lean to tile or a neatly trimmed roof, against the thatch the note calls probable. Both readings stay; a question for the lead, since it changes the kit part.
- **pale band below the farmsteads: water or a low flat** `S1.water.wash_band_34400`: stations +416.6 to +371.1; side 4.6 m S to 12.5 m S. A pale band of blank silk across the lower middle of the silk, between the dark bank below the farmsteads' terrace and the dark near bank at the bottom: either the stream running on west from the bridge, or a low pale flat of bare ground. Wide in the west (u 32850-34150), pinched at u ~33450-33650 and at the rocks (u 34150-34850), and wide again from u ~34650 east to the stream at the bridge. Note: BOTH READINGS (the lead or the user decides; the builder must not settle it silently). WATER: it is blank silk like the stream at the bridge (S1.water.stream_36500), it runs on from it at u ~36000, the land tongues S1.grd.spit_36150 and S1.grd.spit_36225 point east into it, and the scholars put water at the scroll's start; the great willows west of my range (隋堤烟柳, the Sui embankment) would then stand along its bank. GROUND: no ripple lines are drawn anywhere in it; the big willow at u ~32620-32820 west of my range has its roots at v ~1560-1600 on the band's south edge as if standing on its surface; and the restyle draws dry ground (it draws dry ground everywhere, though). shore_n: the foot of the dark bank S1.grd.bank_33650 (u 32850-34450), est round that bank's east end (u 34450-34650), then the foot of S1.grd.bank_35200 (u 34650-35700) and the south edge of the land tongue S1.grd.spit_36225 (u 35800-36000). shore_s: the top of the near bank S1.grd.bank_south_34400. At u 34150-34850 the two banks almost touch (v 1490-1505): only a thin pale strip connects the two halves. West of u 33000 it continues into structures_w's range (v ~1490-1600), where no water entity is recorded; structures_w notes the other way round that the DARK band v 1450-1600 at u 33100-33600 might be water or a hollow. Seen at zoom 3 (insp_lens.jpg) that dark band is made of tongue-shaped wash strokes pointing into the pale silk, like the land tongues by the bridge, so I read the pale silk as the water and the dark as land. MERGE: west of u 33000 nothing continues this band: structures_w recorded the ground there as banks (S1.grd.bank_32905, foot v 1486, and S1.grd.bank_32700, v 1580-1612, which runs inside this band's v range), and it reads the DARK band (v 1450-1600 from 32850 east) as the possible water, where this entity takes the PALE band. Water against ground is the ground pilot's open question for the user; the builder must not settle it silently. SECOND PASS (start), u 35400-36000 only: the lowest dark wash stroke above the band, which is the west end of S1.grd.spit_36225's layer, ends at v ~1385-1395 from 35500 to 35850. The step to the paler silk below measures 1392 at 35500 (weak, 96 -> 113), 1385-1390 at 35560-35700 (101 -> 120-125) and 1393 at 35780. So the shore is about 60 px lower there than the 1330 mapped from S1.grd.bank_35200's foot. At 35420 the dark ends at 1342. EXTENT: the band is pale only east of about 35650. At 35450-35600 the silk below v 1340 is one even mid-tone (104-115) down to the silk's edge, with no pale band and no near bank (see S1.grd.bank_south_34400). In this range the band's south shore (35000-35900) is drawn on no stroke. That is more evidence for the water-or-ground question: the pale band does not visibly join the stream's water west of about 35650. FRAME AGENT: shore_s's last two points move onto the near bank's top as the second pass (start) measured it, (35900, 1590) and (36000, 1582) (were 1592 est at both), so this band's south shore meets S1.water.stream_36500's shore_s at u 36000.

BOTH READINGS stay: the builder settles posts-and-beam (a frame) or a lean-to as a choice. t6's props under it: S1.prop.long_plank_shed_35060, S1.prop.jar_shed_35058, S1.fig.figure_in_shed_35108. (1) The strokes are tinted indigo, the garment colour; the west post at 35057 and the forked pole at 35150 are plain grey-black ink. (2) No shaft runs on below v ~800 to the ground at u 35092: the plank line at v 801-805 crosses plain silk there. - **brushwood fence (or hedge) in front of the west farmstead** `S1.str.fence_brush_33760`: stations +399.6 to +390.7; side 0.8 m N to 0.5 m N. A long band of dense crossed hatching in front of the west farmstead's houses, from u ~33395 to ~34120, read as a brushwood (wattle) fence or a hedge; its west end rises into a rounded hump. It hides the houses' walls. Note: Foot line read at zoom 1.7-2.2 (fB.jpg, fC.jpg, g_33280.jpg); top v ~780-830. Height 60-110 px (foot to top), i.e. tall for a fence: SCALE CUE. READINGS: (a) a brushwood fence or hedge round the farmstead (the crossed short strokes differ from the smoother roof wash above them); (b) the lower thatch of the houses coming down almost to the ground. The rounded HUMP at its west end (u 33400-33540, top (33491, 793)) is drawn the same way; the restyle paints it as a golden haystack: if it is a free-standing straw stack, it is t5's prop (listed in my report). MERGE: t4's "bush at 33375-33450, v 813-905" (left to t5) is this belt's west end; t5 reads the hatched front slopes above it as the thatch of S1.bld.farmhouse_back_33450 and S1.bld.farmhouse_gable_33670, and recorded no straw stack, so the hump (top (33491, 793)) stays part of this belt or of those roofs; the restyle's golden haystack is not recorded. Second pass: settle hump against thatch at zoom 2. SECOND PASS (farms), the hump: it is not a free-standing stack. At zoom 1.7-2.6 (hump_hp.jpg, hump_z.jpg, h1_norm.jpg) its outline is one crisp, smooth line from (33430, 845) up through (33500, 800) to (33560, 778), where it meets S1.bld.farmhouse_gable_33670's west eave corner (33575, 772). The surface inside is the same short slanted dashes as the rest of the band and as the thatch on the gable house's east slope (33680-33760, v 780-850), with no boundary between them. The band's lower edge is a straight dark line at v 867-881 (33430-33780), with a paler strip below it (v 881-899) and then the ground line. Nothing separates the hump from the band, so no prop is proposed. The evidence leans two ways. In the WEST (33400-33780) it favours reading (b): the smooth outline and the thatch dashes make the hump the west hip of a low thatched roof, with its eave at v ~875 (the front slope of the gable house's roof, or a low thatched wing in front of S1.bld.farmhouse_back_33450). In the EAST (33780-34150) it favours reading (a): S1.bld.farmhouse_thatched_33955's east hip ends at a corner post at (34142, 848-887) (see that entity), while the band runs on below the corner to v ~890, so there it stands in front of the wall line. The builder must not settle this silently. If the lead takes (b) for the west part, this line would start at u ~33780, and the west part becomes roof (a building entity, or eaves of S1.bld.farmhouse_gable_33670 lowered to v ~875).

- **the vegetable field (菜地)** `S1.grd.field_vegetable_29790`: stations +344.8 to +317.6; side 5.6 m S to 11.4 m S; footprint 5.5 m (front) × 5.3 m (deep) (est: se, nw). A slightly raised plot south of the village ground, west of the farmhouse, planted with young bare trees: 13 trunk feet and a faint fourteenth, each its own tree entity (S1.tree.young_bare_29558 to S1.tree.young_bare_29968), whose low crab-claw crowns fill the plot (the second pass counted the feet; "25-35 small plants" was a reading of these crowns); a pale pointed rock (S1.grd.rock_29745) stands at its north-west corner. Note: North edge v 1116-1130 from the rock (29790) to 30000 (zoom 1.6); south edge = the top of the bank S1.grd.bank_29750 (v 1330-1350); west end at about 29550-29600 where the plants start; east end at the farmhouse wing (30000-30056). ROWS: the plants stand in loose bands at about v 1140-1200, 1200-1280 and 1280-1350, running roughly along the scroll (dv/du about 0); no furrow or row line is drawn, so the field gives NO receding edge for the frame. The plants are painted as tiny bare shrubs (the restyle too): vegetables (pin 5 名画记: 菜地) or a nursery of saplings. t1 records the taller ones as 13 young bare trees (S1.tree.young_bare_29558 ... S1.tree.young_bare_29948, bases v 1222-1400): this entity is the plot's ground only, never a second record of its plants; whether they are a crop or a grove is the merge's and the user's call (BOTH READINGS). t1's post S1.prop.post_crossbar_29862 stands at its north edge. MERGE: the plants are counted two ways: t1 records 13 young bare trees with trunks 90-180 px tall (S1.tree.young_bare_29558 ... _29948, count good to about +-2); this entity's what describes 25-35 small twiggy plants 30-70 px tall in 3-4 loose bands. On the merge's zoom-1.6 crop the plot is a dense mass of small bare crab-claw crowns over thin trunks (v 1150-1350), the trunks clearer in its east half. SECOND PASS (west): trunk feet counted on column darkness profiles (red channel, 20 px bands of v from 1180 to 1380): 13 trunks, the same as t1's trees (u 29558, 29647-29661, 29733-29738, 29746-29756, 29780, 29794-29796, 29837, 29860-29862, 29879-29885, 29899-29905, 29915-29920, 29933, 29950), plus a faint fourteenth at 29966-29975 (proposed, S1.tree.young_bare_29968, low). Between them the profiles show no further stems with feet: the '25-35 small plants' are these trees' low crab-claw crowns and branches (the middle group's trees are only 90-170 px tall, so their crowns fill the band v 1250-1350). The planting to build is the 13 (or 14) trees; crop or grove of saplings stays the user's call.

SCALE CUES: veranda posts about 50 px from eave to floor (v 1050-1100) and spaced about 12-25 px (balusters) - small against the travellers below (about 180 px tall at v 1550-1630) and the walker at 29200 (about 105 px at v 1060): the painter shrinks the house.
- **banked far fields behind the chase field, with a diagonal edge (low confidence)** `S1.grd.banked_fields_30400`: stations +331.5 to +331.5; side 13.7 m N to 10.0 m N. Far fields on the rising ground north of the raised plot S1.grd.field_30430 and its dotted ridge, behind the chase field: level field banks drawn as horizontal ink lines about 30 px of v apart, crossed by a long diagonal line running down to the south-east to the dotted ridge (a raised path or a field's edge), the same field pattern as S1.grd.terraced_fields_29450 west of the willows. Note: SECOND PASS (west): not recorded; the diagonal was named only in notes: t2's on S1.tree.willow_far_30500 ('a slanting bank line ((30406, 256) -> (30583, 379))'; a line profile finds no stroke on its west half, which lies 30-36 px above the measured diag, and its east half within about 10 px of it) and structures_w's on S1.grd.field_30430 ('A long faint diagonal (30200,230)->(30550,390) above it may be another field edge or a willow branch (not recorded)'). Measured with row darkness profiles (red channel, mean of 30-40 px column bands) and line profiles (mean red along a line against parallel lines 2-16 px off): edge_v334 is the strongest (on the line 118-134 against 147-155 either side from u 30260 to 30420, fading by 30460); edge_v364 is clear from 30260 to 30500 (122-135 against 144-151); edge_v303 is weaker (133-140 against 148-155, u 30300-30420). The diagonal is clear from u 30410 to 30570 (offset 0 to +8 px: 133-141 against 143-156) and weaker from 30290 to 30410 (140-143 against 150-154; may be willow shoots there, so the first point is est); it ends on the dotted ridge at about (30550, 385). The straight long diagonal (30200,230)->(30550,390) that the note gives has no line under it (profile flat). Which side of each bank is higher is not drawn: north higher, as everywhere in S1 (a few px each, far). No receding edge for the frame: the field lines run along the scroll and the diagonal slopes +0.63 down to the right (north-west to south-east on the ground, as S1.road.path_29640 does at +0.9). The restyle draws terraced strips with a sloping path edge here (u 30290-30570, v 210-410). For the builder: one field system with S1.grd.terraced_fields_29450 (lines every 30-40 px there too), seen again east of the twin willow's crown.
- **raised field behind the chase field** `S1.grd.field_30430`: stations +331.5 to +331.5; side 11.5 m N to 7.2 m N. A slightly raised plot of ground behind the chase field's north bank, its north edge a ridge drawn as a dotted line of small clumps (plants or clods), its west end a short slope. Note: The dotted ridge v 372-385 (u 30290-30550) and the bank S1.grd.bank_30300 (top v 498-508 there) bound a light plot about 120 px deep. A field (the clumps a crop row) or a raised strip of ground: both readings. The banked fields above it, with a diagonal edge from about (30370, 266) down to the ridge at (30550, 381), are S1.grd.banked_fields_30400 (second pass). SECOND PASS (west): east of u 30370 the clumps' dark centres lie 8-14 px below the recorded ridge (line profiles: darkest at +8 to +14 px at 30370-30450 and +4 to +10 px at 30450-30550); the west end at the bump (30300-30330, 365-375) is unchanged.
- **bank on the north side of the chase field** `S1.grd.bank_30300`: stations +331.5 to +329.3; side 11.7 m N to 4.9 m N. A long bank rising gently to the east along the north side of the open field where the runaway horse and its chasers run; willows stand along its top at both ends and a raised field (S1.grd.field_30430) lies behind it. Note: Read at zoom 1 (labels legible): the upper dark stroke (29980,560)->(30650,470) and the lower dark band (30050,580)->(30600,600). Higher side north. Height about 35-60 px of v between top and foot; the willow at (30650,520) stands on its top. East of 30650 it is the bank under the willows west of the party's track (v 495-547). MERGE: the small marks below its foot at (30090-30113, v 590-627) that t1 left as "small rocks or a stump" and t2 as "stones or moss dots" are a few faint dots on a zoom-3 crop: moss dots, no entity.
- **banked fields at the top of the silk** `S1.grd.terraced_fields_29450`: stations +331.5 to +319.8; side 16.5 m N to 9.3 m N. Far fields at the top of the silk behind the village: long level strips divided by low banks or ridges (horizontal ink lines every 30-40 px of v), crossed by the diagonal raised path S1.road.path_29640; trees and small far figures with a dark animal stand on the strip at v 120-150. Note: Read at zoom 1-2 with raised contrast. Each edge_* line is one field bank or ridge, named by its v. Which side is higher is not drawn; read as fields stepping up to the north (up the silk), each step a few px (far: the figures standing on edge_v155's strip are 32-47 px tall, so a low bank there is about 5-10 px). BOTH READINGS: terraced fields (the restyle draws stone-faced terraces) or flat fields with raised ridges between them; the strokes give only the lines. The lines continue west of the seam to about 28950 (edge_v57 at (28960,58), edge_v155 at (28950,148)): S2's ground. diag_ne (29740,30)->(29850,130) is a second diagonal line parallel to the path, perhaps the fields' east boundary. No receding (up-right) field edge is drawn. MERGE (cross-key seam check against map/strokes/S2.json, u 28750-29350): edge_v155 starts at (29050, 152), 2 px from S2.grd.bank_lines_top_28900's edge_n end (29050, 150): the same stroke, already trimmed at the seam on both sides. S2's lower line (edge_s, ending (29050, 192) est; its note says it runs to (29073, 192)) has no S1 line east of the seam. edge_v57 runs on west of the seam to about (28960, 58), where S2 has no line: a seam note for S2. SECOND PASS (west): lines added and moved from row darkness profiles (red channel, 30-40 px column bands, local minima against a running background) and line profiles (mean red along a line against parallel lines 2-16 px off), checked on zoom 1.6-2.2 colour and blurred-grey crops. ADDED near the seam, where nothing was recorded between v 57 and v 152 east of 29050: edge_v70w (u 29100-29380, on the line 121-128 against 130-147), edge_v95w (29100-29330, 127-132 against 134-148) and edge_v128w (a band v 126-141, its top edge from (29050, 110) to (29215, 133); it continues the curved line S2's t12 noted at (28999, 83)->(29079, 126), which a line profile confirms west of the seam at (29000, 81)->(29050, 106): S2's side, not recorded by S2). ADDED east: edge_v63e (29790-30130, 135 against 147, sloping up to the east; it may run on to about (30230, 50) behind the new far tree S1.tree.far_tree_top_30297). EXTENDED east past the silk crease at u ~29845: edge_v85 to (30060, 88) (depth 13-21 at u 29860-30010) and edge_v120 to (30060, 112) (weaker, depth 8-11). MOVED: edge_v185 -> edge_v177 (no stroke on the old line, 147 against 142-150; the stroke is at v 176-177, 140 against 148) and edge_v265 -> edge_v282 (no stroke on the old line: 140 against 133-142; the stroke is at v 279-286 from u 29560 to 29830, depth 21-30, broken where S1.road.path_29640 crosses it at u 29620-29690). Both moved lines lie partly under willow shoots (S1.tree.willow_stall_c_29300, S1.tree.willow_east_29845); the profiles were read in bands on both sides of the shoots. Not changed: edge_v57 (its stroke is faint; the darkest rows at 29050-29150 are v 43-48 and 67-69, both weak), edge_v300 (no clear stroke on it or 18 px below; shoots), edge_v152 and edge_v215 (within 5 px). The far figures stand on these strips: the pair at v 118-122 on the strip between edge_v95w and edge_v128w; S1.fig.far_figure_29337 (v 141) and the group at v 143-145 on the strip between edge_v128w's line and edge_v155. FRAME (cross-key seam check): edge_v155's first point takes S2.grd.bank_lines_top_28900 edge_n's last point exactly, (29050, 150) (was (29050, 152)), so the line joins across the seam; copied from map/strokes/S2.json on every run.
- **raised field path across the far fields** `S1.road.path_29640`: stations +330.6 to +329.5; side 13.7 m N to 9.9 m N. A straight raised path (or a bank between fields) running diagonally down across the far banked fields at the top of the silk, from about (29515,148) to (29720,330), drawn as two or three parallel lines. Note: Slope about +0.9 (down to the right): it runs north-west to south-east on the ground, not straight back, so it gives no dx knot. Its top end meets the field bank S1.grd.terraced_fields_29450 edge_v155 where the far figures and the dark animal stand (t1's); its lower end fades behind the stall shed's willows at about v 330. The restyle reads a raised path across terraced fields (right on the form).
- **small thatched shed behind the stall's east end** `S1.bld.stall_back_shed_29860`: stations +328.7 to +326.2; side 4.8 m N to 4.0 m N; footprint 1.5 m (front) × 1.1 m (deep); heights sw_eave 1.48 m, se_eave 1.53 m; front faces S (est: sw, se, ne, nw). A second, smaller thatched roof behind (north of) the east end of the stall shed, its eave a row of hanging thatch ends; a big willow trunk leans across its front, and under its eave the silk is blank (no wall drawn). Note: Read at zoom 2-2.8 with raised contrast: thatch ends at v 505-530 from u 29795 to 29900 under a top line at v 485-490 (29790-29910); its eave sits 20-25 px higher on the silk than the main shed's (v 548), so it stands a little further back. THATCH. The restyle makes it a white-walled house with a blue tile roof (29764-29960): the roof is painted, the walls and the tiles are not. Base corners are placeholders: the front foot taken about 115 px below the eave, by the main shed's eave height (the willow's roots at v 630-650 are there).
- **village stall shed (thatched hip roof, open front)** `S1.bld.stall_shed_29600`: stations +327.2 to +319.6; side 4.8 m N to 3.0 m N; footprint 5.9 m (front) × 1.5 m (deep); heights sw_eave 1.58 m, se_eave 1.48 m; front faces S (est: sw, se, ne, nw). The long shed of the village tea-and-food stall where the canal market begins: a big thatched hip roof (hatched straw strokes; the front eave a dashed row of thatch ends) on posts, the front open. At its west end customers sit at a table under the eave (the lower roof S1.bld.stall_annex_29350); between the posts at u 29486-29599 a board or lattice fascia hangs under the eave; east of it the front under the eave is blank silk (no wall drawn: open, or a pale plastered wall). A second, smaller thatched roof stands behind its east end (S1.bld.stall_back_shed_29860). Note: THATCH, not tile: the roof is filled with hatched straw strokes and its eave is a row of short hanging thatch ends at v 539-550 from u 29374 to 29825 (the restyle draws blue tile: wrong). ROOF (zoom 2, raised contrast): the ridge is a line at v 420 running west from 29675 (its west end lost in the willow branches); the east end is a HIP: a line from the ridge end (29675,420) down to the front eave's east corner (29825,548) (slope +0.85, a hip line, not a receding edge). FRONT: one post read clearly at u 29486 (v 551-664); the fascia board 29486-29599 at v 551-570; the east end edge drops from (29805,550) to (29820,620). Ground line: the post foot (29486,664) and t1's counter S1.prop.stall_counter_29430 (feet v 664-666); the other bays and the sw/se corners are est on that line. DEPTH: not painted; the back corners are placeholders 62 px of v behind the front (about 1.2 m at 51.25 px/m: the builder settles the depth as choice:). The long horizontal line at v 361-367 (u 29599-29705) above the roof is a far field bank (S1.grd.terraced_fields_29450 edge_v362), not the ridge. SCALE CUES: front eave about 120 px over the ground (eave v 545, ground v 665); fascia 113 px long; eave run 451 px (29374-29825); ridge 128 px above the eave. SECOND PASS (west): the slats over the counter (u 29437-29490, v 553-597) are this shed's lattice front running on west of the post at u 29486 (vertical slats from the eave to the counter's top, the same as the fascia lattice at 29486-29599, v 551-570); a few dark knots among them at v 565-590 (u 29445-29480) may be small goods on the counter. No entity (as the merge decided); build the lattice from 29437.
- **low bank in front of the stall's yard** `S1.grd.bank_29590`: stations +325.7 to +317.3; side 1.4 m N to on the centre line. A soft ink-wash step along the front of the stall's yard, just south of where the buffalo lies and the ox stands: the yard is a little higher than the open ground south of it. Note: A darker wash band v 760-800 with a line at v 775-785, u 29280-29900 (zoom 1, raised contrast). Higher side north. Height est 10-20 px (no post or figure stands on the step). Could be only shading of the ground: conf low.
- **stall shed, lower west roof (customers' end)** `S1.bld.stall_annex_29350`: stations +321.6 to +318.2; side 4.6 m N to 2.7 m N; footprint 1.4 m (front) × 2.2 m (deep); heights sw_eave 1.65 m, se_eave 1.62 m; front faces S (est: sw, se, ne, nw). A lower roof at the west end of the stall shed, over the seated customers: its top at about v 460 and its eave at v 535-540, in line with the main shed's eave; the west end is behind the two willow trunks over the stall. Open front, customers seated at a table under it. Note: Read at zoom 2.4: a roof slope with hatching from about (29250,463) down to (29367,534) and an eave line at v 535-540 from 29262 to 29367, lower than the main shed's roof (ridge at v 420). It may be a lean-to or the main roof's lower west wing; recorded as its own roof so the builder can make the step. A small triangle at (29300-29325, 409-437) above it may be a gable end or a willow branch. Ground corners est on the main shed's front line (v 662-665); back corners placeholders, 62-67 px of v behind as for the main shed. SCALE CUE: eave about 125 px over the ground line.
- **pale pointed rock at the field's north-west corner** `S1.grd.rock_29745`: stations +321.1 to +319.7; side 5.5 m S to 6.7 m S; footprint 1.1 m (front) × 1.0 m (deep); heights sw_top 0.68 m, se_top 0.71 m (est: ne, nw). A pale, rounded upright mass about 90 px wide and 160-180 px tall at the north-west corner of the vegetable field, outlined with short dark hatched strokes on its west side. Note: BOTH READINGS (zoom 3): (a) a rock or earth mound with textured strokes; (b) more likely a figure in a hooded cloak seen from behind (a dome-shaped hood 35 px wide at v 1043-1060, shoulders widening to 75 px by v 1100, a cross line like a belt at v 1100, the lower body hidden by the plants), in scale with the man at the post beside it (t1's S1.fig.man_at_post_29812, head v 968); the restyle draws a man in a blue robe there. t1 recorded the same mass as the prop S1.prop.rock_or_heap_29745 (rock, earth mound or straw heap; outline u 29704-29788, v 1039-1147): the merge keeps ONE of the two (this grd if a rock or mound, t1's prop if a heap, a figure of t1's if a hooded man). Front foot taken at v 1145-1147 as t1 reads it (the field's plants start there); back corners placeholders. MERGE: t1 recorded the same mass as the prop S1.prop.rock_or_heap_29745 (rock, earth mound or straw heap; its west face's hatching "like rock texture more than straw"); that prop is retired and this entity kept. On the merge's zoom 2.5-4 crops it reads as an upright pointed rock: the shaded west face is laid in with diagonal texture strokes, and no head, face, hands or feet are drawn. SECOND PASS (west): a rock; reading (b) is dropped. At zoom 2-3 the west face is laid in with short parallel diagonal texture strokes (rock texture), the outline rises to a pointed, slightly west-leaning summit at (29757, 1043) and runs down into the field's ground with no hem, feet or hands, and no face or hood opening is drawn. As a figure it would stand 104 px from summit to foot (v 1043-1147) beside the 146 px man at the post at the same foot level: too short for a standing adult. The restyle's man in blue here is invented.

## Everything, east to west

### Stations +445.0 to +440.0

- **pole leaning against the trees** `S1.prop.leaning_pole_37075`: station +440.6, 2.8 m N; 9.4 m E of small plank bridge (草桥). A straight pole or cut timber, about as thick as a branch, leaning across the slender tree's trunk: its foot on the ground against the west side of the big forked tree's trunk, its squared upper end sticking out north-west past the slender tree. Left there by someone. (sections.md §2: mist, sparse trees and water at the very start) Nearest: tall slender bare tree with its crown bent west 1.3 m W, big forked bare elm-like tree at the scroll's start 2.5 m S, charcoal driver with a switch, at the train's east end 5.2 m W. [est: base; identification med] Note: Parallel edges about 10-12 px apart, from the squared upper end at (36986-36999, 694-702) to the foot at (37072-37080, 821-830), crossing S1.tree.slender_bare_37018's trunk at about (37035, 750); 140 px long. BOTH READINGS: a cut pole or timber leaned on the tree (taken: the squared end and straight edges) or a dead limb broken off S1.tree.elm_bare_37140 and fallen against the slender tree. The foot meets the big tree's trunk, so its ground point is est. The line is elevated (slope 1.54), not a ground edge.

### Stations +440.0 to +435.0

- **big forked bare elm-like tree at the scroll's start** `S1.tree.elm_bare_37140`: station +439.9, 0.5 m N; 8 m E of small plank bridge (草桥); about 7.34 m tall; crown 6.66 m wide. A big bare elm-like tree (elm_claw form) on the bank at the scroll's start: a thick gnarled trunk from spreading roots forks low into three main limbs, one rising north-west, one up to the crown's top and a long one reaching far east toward the silk's edge, all ending in crab-claw twig clusters; a short cut or broken limb stub juts from a limb at about (37235, 600). No leaves. A pole leans against the trees at its foot. (sections.md §2: mist, sparse trees and water at the very start) Nearest: tall slender bare tree with its crown bent west 2 m N, pole leaning against the trees 2.5 m N, charcoal driver with a switch, at the train's east end 4.2 m W. Note: Trunk u 37050-37140 at v 813-880 going down to roots u 37100-37205 at v 930-960; the fork at about (37110, 800). The low twigs east of the fork (u 37240-37330, v 740-820) are its low east limb, not a separate shrub. The crown's east tips reach u 37568, below and beside the seals (u 37250-37500, v 100-320; u 37410-37540, v 800-1000), which are never recorded. Painted height 565 px.
- **tall slender bare tree with its crown bent west** `S1.tree.slender_bare_37018`: station +439.0, 2.3 m N; 8.1 m E of small plank bridge (草桥); about 7.92 m tall; crown 3.64 m wide. A tall slender bare tree east of the train: a thin dark trunk rising almost straight from the bank to a small crown of crab-claw twigs, whose limbs bend over and reach far west (slender form, like its card 2, 'a long curve to the west'); no leaves. The leaning pole S1.prop.leaning_pole_37075 rests across its trunk. (sections.md §2: mist, sparse trees and water at the very start) Nearest: pole leaning against the trees 1.3 m E, big forked bare elm-like tree at the scroll's start 2 m S, charcoal driver with a switch, at the train's east end 3.9 m W. Note: Trunk u 37013-37047 from v 330 down to its foot at about v 850, where the bank's ink wash meets it; crown u 36835-37115, v 242-370. Painted height 608 px. SECOND PASS (start): the base is at the trunk's centre, not on its east outline. Row profiles (1-px columns, 10 px bands) find the trunk's outlines at u 36995-37000 and 37020-37025 for v 800-830. Below that the west outline flares out to a root spread at 36965-36985 (v 840-860), and the east outline stays at 37015-37020. Foot centre about (37000, 852); the mapped 37018 is the east outline. Small (18 px), for consistency with how the other feet were taken.
- **charcoal driver with a switch, at the train's east end** `S1.fig.charcoal_driver_36797`: station +435.7, on the centre line; 4.2 m E of small plank bridge (草桥); walking; faces W. Adult man, the charcoal train's driver (赶脚的): a wide round-brimmed hat, a short jacket belted over trousers, legs apart; he leans west toward the leading donkey with his right arm stretched to the top of its load (holding the load or its lead) and holds a long switch out to the east in his left hand, its tip hanging down. His face, under the hat, is turned west toward the donkeys. (名画记: 赶驴入城 (pin 1)) Nearest: charcoal donkey D5, the leader, with woven panniers 1 m W, charcoal donkey D4, head up and ears pricked 2.1 m W, pale far tree behind the big bare tree 3.6 m NW. Note: Hat brim u 36780-36810, v 831-842; feet at (36783, 921) and (36810, 928); right hand at (36755, 860) on D5's load; left hand at about (36840, 855), the switch running on to (36853, 900). Painted height 94 px at foot v 925 (diminution pair). BOTH READINGS of his walk: he walks east in front of the train leading D5 and looks back (the donkeys' heads point east), or he drives the train from behind toward the west and the city (赶驴入城, the scholarship's reading); facing 200 = his body and face turned to the donkeys. The restyle paints 6-8 men in blue round the train: only he and S1.fig.charcoal_driver_36250 are painted.

### Stations +435.0 to +430.0

- **charcoal donkey D5, the leader, with woven panniers** `S1.ani.donkey_charcoal_36745`: station +434.8, 0.4 m S; 3.2 m E of small plank bridge (草桥); walking; faces S; 0.34 m long. The leading donkey, carrying two big hatched (woven wicker) panniers of charcoal, one on each side, seen nearly end-on with its head lowered between them toward the viewer; the east driver's right hand rests on the top of its load. (名画记: 赶驴入城 (pin 1)) Nearest: charcoal driver with a switch, at the train's east end 1 m E, charcoal donkey D4, head up and ears pricked 1.2 m NW, charcoal donkey D3, head hanging low by the big tree 2.9 m W. [identification med; part: head between the panniers] Note: Panniers u 36697-36773, v 857-913 (76 px across, hatched like wickerwork, dark charcoal heaped at the top, v 857-870); legs end at (36713, 932), (36730, 937), (36748, 940) and (36760, 947). BOTH READINGS (conf low on the facing): the dark upright shape between the panniers (u 36732-36747, v 875-935) is its lowered head seen from the front (facing the viewer and a little east, taken) or its rump and tail seen from behind (facing north-west). D4's muzzle touches its west pannier.
- **charcoal donkey D4, head up and ears pricked** `S1.ani.donkey_charcoal_36666`: station +433.7, on the centre line; 2.7 m NE of small plank bridge (草桥); walking; faces E; 0.95 m long. A donkey with a dark pannier load, head raised and both long ears pricked up at its east end, its muzzle against the west side of D5's panniers; the big bare tree's trunk hides the middle of its body. (名画记: 赶驴入城 (pin 1)) Nearest: charcoal donkey D5, the leader, with woven panniers 1.2 m SE, charcoal donkey D3, head hanging low by the big tree 1.7 m W, pale far tree behind the big bare tree 2 m NW. [part: the trunk of S1.tree.elm_bare_36660 hides its hind half] Note: Head u 36677-36700, v 830-872 (ear tips v 830, muzzle at (36697, 868)); the neck rises from the body's east end (u 36650-36670); body and pannier u 36620-36673, v 833-883 east of the trunk; front legs end at (36658, 913) and (36675, 917); two hind hooves at u 36583-36590, v 903-910 just west of the trunk. The setup brief read it as 'head turned'; on the strokes the whole body faces east like D2 and D3, so it is not turned back. SCALE CUE: 87 px from the hooves to the ear tips; 115 px from the hind hooves to the muzzle.
- **big bare elm-like tree at the small bridge's east end** `S1.tree.elm_bare_36660`: station +433.0, 3.6 m S; 0.8 m E of small plank bridge (草桥); about 9.39 m tall; crown 8.7 m wide. A big old bare tree (elm-like, elm_claw form): a thick gnarled trunk rises from roots spread over the stream bank at the small bridge's east end and leans north-west, forking at about v 700 into a limb up to the crown's top, a long low limb sweeping west over the charcoal train and a limb to the east; every branch ends in dark crab-claw twig clusters, no leaves. The donkey train passes behind (north of) its trunk. (sections.md §2: mist, sparse trees and water at the very start) Nearest: charcoal donkey D4, head up and ears pricked 3.6 m NE, charcoal donkey D3, head hanging low by the big tree 3.7 m N, upright pole at the small boat's east end (mooring pole?) 3.7 m W. Note: Trunk u 36610-36653 at v 940-1000; roots over the bank from u 36630 to 36770 at v 1040-1095; base at the trunk's foot. The bridge deck's east end meets the trunk at about (36640, 1030) (structures_e's). Painted height 723 px. The west limb's twigs reach about u 36160 (v 550-600), over t7's range.
- **charcoal donkey D3, head hanging low by the big tree** `S1.ani.donkey_charcoal_36508`: station +432.0, on the centre line; 2.6 m NE of small plank bridge (草桥); walking; faces SE; 0.82 m long. A donkey with a basket of charcoal on its back, its head and neck hanging low at its east end against the big bare tree's trunk; walking in the file between D2 and D4. (名画记: 赶驴入城 (pin 1)) Nearest: charcoal donkey D2, head hanging low 1.1 m NW, pale far tree behind the big bare tree 1.4 m NE, charcoal donkey D4, head up and ears pricked 1.7 m E. [identification med; faded] Note: Rounded rump at the west end (u 36437-36451, v 821-845); load u 36471-36511, v 807-825; head and neck lowered to u 36505-36540, v 835-863, among the trunk lines of S1.tree.elm_bare_36660 (other reading: its tail, facing west: conf med on the facing). Legs end at (36443, 885), (36458, 880), (36498, 880) and (36517, 885). SCALE CUE: 103 px from rump to muzzle, 78 px from the load's top to the hooves.
- **upright pole at the small boat's east end (mooring pole?)** `S1.prop.mooring_pole_36444`: station +432.0, 6.2 m S; H -0.7 m (stream); 1.8 m S of small plank bridge (草桥). A thin, straight, upright pole standing in the stream at the pointed east end of the small empty boat (S1.veh.boat_sampan_36440), rising about 60 px above the boat's end: the boat is tied to it, a pole planted in the stream bed (the notes' 'mooring rope to the bridge post' is the other reading). Nearest: small empty boat (sampan) moored under the small bridge 1.6 m W, big bare elm-like tree at the small bridge's east end 3.7 m E, tall straight bare tree at the bridge's west end 4.8 m W. [est: base; identification low; faded] Note: SECOND PASS (start): t8 named this line in the boat's `what` and note as 'its mooring rope (or a pole stuck in the stream bed)' running 'up to the foot of a bridge post at about (36445, 1100)', and the merge wrote that into S1.str.small_bridge_36365's note. On the strokes it does not reach a post. Row profiles (1-px columns, 10 px bands, blur 1.2) find a dark line at u 36442-36446 from v 1130 to 1180 (9-15 levels below the band's median), possibly from about 1115 (a broad weak minimum at 36437). There is nothing at v 1070-1110 (depth 3-11 at the band's edge only). The nearest post feet are S1.str.small_bridge_36365's bays (36455, 1063), (36480, 1067), (36491, 1065): 40-60 px of plain silk lie between the line's top and them. It is vertical (no slant toward any post) and straight, and it stops at the boat's end below and at nothing above, so it is not a silk crease. A rope tied to a post would reach the post and slant, so the reading taken is a pole planted in the stream bed beside the boat's east end, like the mooring posts S2 and S3 record standing in the water (S2.prop.mooring_post_23126, S3.prop.mooring_post_17702: support water; here the stream). Base est at the waterline at the boat's end; its foot is under water. If the lead keeps the rope reading, decline this and correct the boat's and the bridge's notes instead (the 'post foot at (36445, 1100)' has no post: see the changes). Crops: boat_z3.jpg, rope_z5g.jpg, ov_bridge.jpg, bridge_z16.jpg.
- **pale far tree behind the big bare tree** `S1.tree.far_fan_tree_36465`: station +431.4, 1.2 m N; 4 m NE of small plank bridge (草桥); about 8.42 m tall; crown 3.38 m wide. A tall far tree painted in pale ink behind the big bare tree: a straight pale grey trunk and, at the top of the silk, faint wide fans of long fine straight shoots; a far willow with rising fans of shoots (willow_fan) or a pine-like tree (pine_fan). Bare or a faint haze. (sections.md §2: mist, sparse trees and water at the very start) Nearest: charcoal donkey D2, head hanging low 1.2 m W, charcoal donkey D3, head hanging low by the big tree 1.4 m SW, charcoal donkey D1, last in the train (west end) 1.8 m W. [est: base; identification med; part: trunk below v 790 hidden by the donkeys D2 and D3; crown faint] Note: Pale trunk u 36455-36480 from v ~425 down to v ~790, behind D2 and D3; faint fans at u 36445-36536, v 172-218, u 36490-36605, v 286-377 and u 36627-36695, v 323-395. Base est on the ground behind the train. The dark knotted limb crossing its top at (36450-36480, 360-430) is S1.tree.elm_bare_36660's. Other reading: the fans are a separate far crown and the pale trunk a bare pole. The restyle paints a straight trunk here too.
- **charcoal donkey D2, head hanging low** `S1.ani.donkey_charcoal_36410`: station +430.9, on the centre line; 3.5 m NE of small plank bridge (草桥); walking; faces SE; 0.65 m long. A donkey with two baskets heaped with dark charcoal lumps on its pack saddle, its head hanging low at its east end; walking in the file between D1 and D3. (名画记: 赶驴入城 (pin 1)) Nearest: charcoal donkey D1, last in the train (west end) 0.9 m NW, charcoal donkey D3, head hanging low by the big tree 1.1 m SE, pale far tree behind the big bare tree 1.2 m E. [identification med; faded] Note: Load u 36357-36411, v 757-790 (two baskets); body u 36343-36420, v 771-812; the head is a lowered wedge at u 36407-36435, v 800-815, read as the head with the ears forward (other reading: a swishing tail, which would make it face west: conf med on the facing). Legs end at (36351, 829), (36380, 843), (36403, 821) and (36417, 831). SCALE CUE: body 77 px long, 80 px from the load's top to the hooves.
- **charcoal donkey D1, last in the train (west end)** `S1.ani.donkey_charcoal_36323`: station +430.1, on the centre line; 4.1 m NE of small plank bridge (草桥); walking; faces SE; 0.31 m long. A donkey laden with charcoal: two big bulging panniers slung either side of its back, heaped with dark lumps of charcoal; seen almost end-on, so its head does not show. The rear (westmost) donkey of the train, with the faint second driver just west of it. (名画记: 赶驴入城 (pin 1)) Nearest: second charcoal driver at the train's west end (faint) 0.7 m NW, charcoal donkey D2, head hanging low 0.9 m SE, pale far tree behind the big bare tree 1.8 m E. [identification med; part: head hidden (seen end-on, behind D2's rump)] Note: Load u 36283-36343, v 732-790 (two panniers 60 px across, lumpy charcoal tops at v 732-745); thin legs end at (36294, 796), (36315, 802) and (36331, 806). BOTH READINGS of the facing (conf low on it): the pale rounded mass on the west pannier (u 36285-36312, v 752-785, with dark marks like eyes) is either its face seen from the front (facing south-west, toward the viewer) or its pale rump seen from behind (facing south-east after D2, as the other donkeys do); 315 is taken from the file and the head est at the east end. The restyle paints two men in hats here (it reads the two panniers as two men). SCALE CUE: 74 px from the load's top to the hooves. SECOND PASS (start): D1 is a side view like D2, not end-on. Its three legs end 37 px apart along u ((36294, 796), (36315, 802), (36331, 806)), as D2's do (36351-36417), and its load is two bundles along its back, as D2's 'two baskets' are. Its head is the dark lowered shape at its east end, u 36332-36350, v 765-802, between its forelegs and D2's rump (greyscale zoom 6, d1_z6g.jpg; zoom 4, d12_z4.jpg): head and neck hang low, muzzle near v 800, as D2's and D3's do. The pale rounded mass with 'eyes' on its west bundle (36285-36312, 752-785) is the pannier's weave pattern. The restyle reads the two bundles as two men. Facing 330 as D2 and D3, the file's direction. Conf on the facing low -> med.

### Stations +430.0 to +425.0

- **second charcoal driver at the train's west end (faint)** `S1.fig.charcoal_driver_36250`: station +429.3, on the centre line; 4.7 m N of small plank bridge (草桥); walking; faces E. A second driver of the charcoal train, very faint, at its west (rear) end, half hidden behind the low west branches of the big bare tree: an adult man in a short robe or jacket, legs apart as if walking, his right arm stretched east toward the last donkey's load. A working man (charcoal driver, 赶脚的). (名画记: 赶驴入城 (pin 1)) Nearest: charcoal donkey D1, last in the train (west end) 0.7 m SE, charcoal donkey D2, head hanging low 1.6 m SE, far tree with fans of shoots at the top (west) 2 m NE. [identification med; faded; part: behind the twigs of S1.tree.elm_bare_36660's west limb] Note: Outline only, read on contrast-raised crops: body u 36232-36268, v 697-745; feet at about (36240, 766) and (36258, 766); the head top is lost among the twigs (est v ~683); an arm or a stick runs from his shoulder east to about (36275-36320, 702-715), toward D1's panniers. Painted height about 83 px at foot v 766. sections.md counts 2 drivers: this is the second. Other reading: crossing twig strokes only (no head is clear), hence conf low. facing 0 = toward the donkeys (east). The restyle paints two men in hats a little east of here, on D1's panniers: those are not painted. SECOND PASS (start): at zoom 4 (colour and blurred greyscale, wdriver_z4.jpg, wdriver_z4g.jpg) he reads as a figure. Both outlines of a short jacket run at u 36235-36257, v 702-737, with a belt line at v ~737. Two legs stand apart, ending at (36238, 760) and (36258, 765). A roundish head shows at u 36240-36262, v 680-702, crossed by twigs, so the head point stays est. His right arm goes east to (36277, 715). From the hand a thin straight stick runs on east, over D1's west pannier, to about (36320, 705). That is a driving stick held out over the last donkey from behind: a carried prop for the needs list. Facing 0 is right. He drives the train east from its rear.
- **far tree with fans of shoots at the top (west)** `S1.tree.far_fan_tree_36300`: station +429.3, 2.0 m N; 6.2 m NE of small plank bridge (草桥); about 6.6 m tall; crown 2.53 m wide. A far tree near the top of the silk, painted in pale ink: from a head at about (36303, 310) fans of long, fine, straight pale shoots rise and spread over a faint short trunk. A far willow with rising fans of shoots (willow_fan, drawn small and far), or a pine-like tree (pine_fan). Bare or a faint haze. (sections.md §2: mist, sparse trees and water at the very start) Nearest: second charcoal driver at the train's west end (faint) 2 m SW, charcoal donkey D1, last in the train (west end) 2.3 m SW, pale far tree behind the big bare tree 2.4 m S. [est: base; identification med; part: trunk below v 410 hidden by the big bare tree's west limb] Note: Fans u 36245-36450, v 192-345, springing from about (36298-36309, 288-327); a pale trunk u 36292-36309 shows down to v ~410, then the twigs of S1.tree.elm_bare_36660 cover it. Base est: unknown between v 450 and 760, taken on the far ground behind the west driver (v 700). BOUNDARY: base u 36300 is t7's east edge; if t7 also records it, keep one. The fans east of u 36440 may be its east limb or S1.tree.far_fan_tree_36465's.
- **far tree crown with two fan heads** `S1.tree.far_crown_36070`: station +427.2, 0.5 m N; 4.8 m N of small plank bridge (草桥); about 7.51 m tall; crown 1.82 m wide. Far tree at the top of the silk behind the tall bare tree: two (perhaps three) pale fans of fine rising shoots (willow_fan far crown) spring from knobbly heads; its trunk is hidden. (sections.md S1: mist, sparse trees and water at the very start (≈ 35000–37610)) Nearest: far tree crown behind the bare elms 1 m W, second charcoal driver at the train's west end (faint) 2.3 m E, charcoal donkey D1, last in the train (west end) 2.7 m E. [est: base; identification low; part: trunk hidden behind the tall bare tree] Note: Upper fan 36020-36130, v 180-255; lower fan 35990-36100, v 255-340, radiating from about (36050, 310). Its trunk may be the thin straight stem at u 36097-36113 (v 445-740) beside the tall trunk of S1.tree.bare_elm_tall_36070, which S1.tree.bare_elm_twin_35920's note reads as that tree's east stem; base est on the grove's ground line. The silk has a patch of bare weave at (36080-36100, 260-300) and (36036-36071, 344-394): damage, not a nest.
